## Red team 2026-11-07 (docs/red-team/2026-11-07.md)

- [x] 0123 red-team H1: the server DOM accepts any element or attribute name and writes `<style>`/`<script>` text raw; `Box({...{as:"img src=x onerror=alert(1) x"}})` renders an XSS tag on the server; throw on invalid names, escape `--` in comments, check `as` at runtime (repro `installServerDom(); Box({...JSON.parse('{"as":"img src=x onerror=alert(1) x"}')}).outerHTML`) · server, ui · k.server k.ui · G3 · 0073
- [x] 0124 red-team H2 (rfc): E1005 misses a forged artifact: change `data`, recompute `hash`, `keel check` reports nothing because it never compares against the compiler's hashes; the clause "any byte → E1005" is false; recompile and pass `artifacts.hashes`, add a forged-hash test (repro `check([{path:".keel/routes.json",source:canonicalJson({data,hash:hashOf(data)})+"\n"}])` → `[]`) · cli, check, compiler · k.compiler · G3 · 0105
- [x] 0125 red-team M1: `saveStores` updates the in-memory copy before `ctx.storage.set` finishes; a storage rejection gives the client 500 but the write still applies and the next save persists it; concurrent saves can land out of order · server · k.server · G3 · 0054
- [x] 0126 red-team M2: a guard that redirects to a route under its own guarded layout loops forever and `next` grows per hop; no check rule, no server hop cap (repro `GET /login` → 302 `/login?next=%2Flogin`) · router, server, check · k.router · G3 · 0044
- [x] 0127 red-team M3: a production `createApp` without `assets` serves the dev entry (bare `@keel/*` imports) to island pages, so islands silently fail; E1555 at startup · server · k.server · G3 · 0116
- [x] 0128 red-team M4: importing `@keel/ui` or `@keel/runtime-dom/ops` after `installServerDom` throws `document.addEventListener is not a function` (import-order invariant, principle 2) · runtime-dom, server · k.runtime · G3 · 0073
- [x] 0129 red-team M5 (rfc): E1601 is silenced by `const D=Date; D.now()`, ``Date[`now`]()``, `const {random}=Math`; `eval`/`new Function` never flagged; E1002 misses `Reflect.set(el,'innerHTML',x)` and `DOMParser` · check, compiler · k.check · G3 · 0076
- [x] 0130 red-team M6: k.services, k.errors, k.testing, k.scaffold, std.geo, std.brands marked `conformance-covered` while the ledger shows every clause unproven; fix headers and gate status by the ledger (rfc:0049 in progress: E1707) · tooling, spec, cli · k.testing · G3 · 0034
- [x] 0131 red-team M7: saved store snapshots keep the whole transaction log incl. caller tokens forever and every request re-parses it; spec a log limit and compact on save · store, server · k.server k.store · G3 · 0054

- [x] 0133 red-team L2: JSX spread on a plain HTML element turns an `onclick` key into an inline handler; `attr` must refuse `on*` names · runtime-dom · k.runtime · G3 · 0084
- [x] 0134 red-team L3: any well-formed `keel-caller` cookie is accepted, so a sibling subdomain can plant a victim's caller; `__Host-` prefix on https · server · k.server · G3 · 0082
- [x] 0135 red-team L4: std-timings stats have no cap on names on the process-lifetime ctx; re-registering the same section throws E1057 · std-timings · std.timings · G3 · 0067

- [x] 0139 red-team (accepted gap, rfc:0050): `.keel/routes.json` is compared only when no `modules/<m>/routes.ts` exists (`artifactHashes` drops it; k.check never runs app code), so a forged self-consistent routes.json passes in every app with a routes manifest; compare it where routes are evaluated (`keel build`/`vite build`) or in a sandboxed evaluator (repro: golden-1 routes.json with `data:{}` and recomputed `hash`; `keel check` → no E1005) · check, compiler · k.compiler · G3 · 0124
- [x] 0140 red-team: server DOM raw text and comments still break out: (a) `rawText` escapes each text node alone, so `s=document.createElement("script"); s.append("<","/script><img src=x onerror=alert(1)>"); s.outerHTML` → `<script></script><img ...>` (style: `"</","style>..."`); (b) a comment child of script/style/textarea/title/xmp keeps `</tag` (`<script><!--</script><img src=x onerror=alert(1)>--></script>`); (c) `svg > style` text is written raw and `<img>` leaves foreign content (`<svg><style><img src=x onerror=alert(1)></style></svg>`); escape the joined text, escape `</` in comments under raw-text/RCDATA parents, raw text for HTML-namespace script/style only · server · k.server · G3 · 0123
- [x] 0141 red-team: Heading `level` is not checked at runtime as Box/Text `as` now are (repro `Heading({...JSON.parse('{"level":"ead","children":"x"}')}).outerHTML` → `<head>x</head>`; `"r"` → `<hr>`); `tagOf` over h1..h6 · ui · k.ui · G3 · 0123

- [x] 0143 red-team: `check()` trusts any `artifacts.hashes` object; `{}` or one missing a compiled name silences E1005 (repro `check([{path:".keel/ids.json",source:canonicalJson({data,hash:hashOf(data)})+"\n"}],{types:false,artifacts:{hashes:{}}})` → `[]`; without the option → E1005); fail closed on a missing name or drop the override · check · k.compiler · G3 · 0124
- [x] 0144 red-team: in a workspace, `keel check` from the root never compares app artifacts: `readProject`/`codeFiles` take `.keel/` only at the cwd root, so `apps/<x>/.keel/*.json` are skipped silently (repro: repo root `keel check apps/inspector` → files 26; in `apps/inspector` → 31) and `generatedEdits` checks nested ones only for self-consistency · cli, check · k.compiler k.cli · G3 · 0124
- [x] 0145 red-team: `nextOf` (rfc:0051 "a guard never carries another origin forward") accepts `/<TAB>/host`, `/<LF>/host`, `/<TAB>\host`; URL parsers strip tab/newline, so the carried `next` is protocol-relative (repro: server/test/guard-redirect.test.ts "not a same-origin path" fixture, `GET /login?next=%2F%09%2Fevil.example` → `302 /?next=%2F%09%2Fevil.example`; `new URL("/\t/evil.example","https://app.example").href` → `https://evil.example/`); refuse C0 controls/space or compare `new URL(next, base).origin`; add control chars to the property test · router · k.router · G3 · 0126
- [x] 0146 red-team: `guardNext` is quadratic in an unencoded nested `next` (`"/login?next=".repeat(n)` needs no encoding; each level re-parses the rest with URLSearchParams and matchUrl): on a guarded redirect-target route 15.6 KB costs 24 ms/req, 7.8 KB 6 ms, a plain 15.6 KB URL 0.4 ms (repro: same fixture, time `app.fetch(new Request("http://localhost"+"/login?next=".repeat(1300)+"/x"))`); cap the depth or un-nest in one scan · router, server · k.router · G3 · 0126
- [x] 0147 red-team (rfc:0052): E1555 fires only when `NODE_ENV` is exactly `production`; Node leaves it unset by default and `prod`/`Production` also skip, so `node server.ts` with default `mode` and no `assets` still serves `/@keel/entry.ts` to island pages, the 0127 repro unchanged in the default environment (repro: server/src/internal/nodeEnv.ts and createApp.ts:55; `createApp({topology,routes,ctx})` with NODE_ENV unset resolves); gate on `mode` alone (tests pass `mode`) or log a diag on the first island document without a build · server · k.server · G3 · 0127
- [x] 0148 red-team: the 409 check covers only changed stores (`changed.find` in saveStores.ts), never ones an action only read: action A reads `app.lists`, writes `app.todos`; overlapping B deletes the list in `app.lists`; both commit (write skew). `open` also loads keys one `await` at a time, so a save of K1+K2 that starts between them gives a request old K1 and new K2 (read skew; a GET renders a state that never existed) (repro: read openStores.ts `for … await load`, saveStores.ts commit); validate every opened key's `from` at commit and load a factory's keys under one `inOrder` · server · k.server · G3 · 0125

## Red team 2026-11-09 (rfc:0070 working tree)
- [x] 0206 red-team (rfc:0070): an `el:<id>#value` entry redacts only `input` events; `keydown` keys pass, so every typed character of a redacted field is in the session (`redactionOf.fire` returns any non-`input` event as is) (repro, happy-dom: `bindStd(ctx,{"@keel/std-sessions":{pay:{redact:["el:pay.card#value"]}}})`, `record(ctx)`, `renderRoute`, `h.fire("pay.card",{type:"keydown",key:"4"})` x4 then input "4111" → events hold keys `4`,`1`,`1`,`1` beside `"xxxx"`); mask one-character keys under an `el:` value entry, as `redactSession` does · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0207 red-team (rfc:0070): password masking reads `el.type` at each event, so a show-password button leaks the password. After `type="text"`, the next `input` reports the whole field value in clear (repro: as 0206 with `Input({id:"pay.pw",type:"password"})`, `pw.type="text"`, `h.fire("pay.pw",{type:"input",value:"hunter2"})` → `"value":"hunter2"` in `stop()`); remember every element once seen as a password (WeakSet in fireOf), or also mask `autocomplete` `current-password`/`new-password` · runtime-dom · k.ctx std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0208 red-team (rfc:0070): an `s.secret` search or param reaches the session in a `nav` href. `redactionOf.nav` rebuilds `href` only when the route has a `route:` entry (`paths.has(key)`), so "s.secret fields are always redacted" is false (repro: route `/pay` with `search: s.object({token: s.secret(s.string()).optional()})`, no redact entries, `h.navigate("/pay?token=SECRET-TOKEN")` → `{"kind":"nav","href":"/pay?token=SECRET-TOKEN"}`); always rebuild href from the redacted params and search · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0209 red-team (rfc:0070): `error.message` is never redacted. `toUnexpected` copies `Error.message` (`new URL(input)`, `JSON.parse`, app text) and `error:` entries reach only `data.*` (E2001 rejects any other path); `redactSession` keeps it too, so a to-test file can hold a recorded string outside `expose` (repro: button throwing `new Error("card 4111-1111")`, click → session `error.message` "card 4111-1111"); mask `message` in `redactSession`, and decide for `record` (an `error:<code>#message` entry, or mask `keel.unexpected` messages) · std-sessions, runtime-dom · std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0210 red-team (rfc:0070): overflow drops the error the session is about. A no-nav rebase that the error itself triggers keeps nothing (only an input is kept), so the upload at that error has `error: null`, `events: []` and the empty fingerprint; a nav rebase cuts earlier errors, so `stop()` names a later error or none (repro: `record(ctx,{limit:1,upload})`, click a throwing button → `session(ctx).taken()[0].session.error === null`, fingerprint `acb6b7ad0850` ≠ live `413c747d49e4`); keep the first error (and its `fire`) across a rebase · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0211 red-team (rfc:0070): a recorder's memory and size are not bounded by `limit`. `raw` (`errors()`) grows by every error forever, and every `input` event carries the whole field, so typing in a 2,000-char textarea makes a session of about 1 MB, over the 256 KiB ingest cap. `void sink.put(...)` drops the `sessions.putFailed` 413, so the session is lost with no signal (read recording.ts `raw.push`, `void sink.put`); cap `raw` at `limit`, cap the bytes of the buffer, and report a failed put · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0093
- [x] 0212 red-team (rfc:0070): `record` on a server-rendered (`node`/`static`) deployable records nothing. Only `createRouter` reports `nav`, and `RESUME_ENTRY` starts no router, so `base` stays undefined: every event is dropped, `upload` never fires and `stop()` is `emptySession`. Inputs queued before resume are re-run through `dispatch()`, not the DOM, so the capture tap never reports them, while their `tx` would be logged (read RESUME_ENTRY.ts, eventQueue.resume, recording.ts `if (base === undefined) return`); report a `nav` at resume from the served route, and report replayed queued events · runtime-dom, compiler, std-sessions · k.ctx std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0213 red-team (rfc:0070): every browser session has `seed: 0`. Both generated entries call `createCtx({mode:"real"})`, `seedOf` gives 0, real `random` is unseeded, and `Session` records no mode, so `replay` under sim seed 0 makes different `random.id`s than the live run. Any session whose inputs target or carry a generated id gives E2003/E2004 (read seedOf.ts, RESUME_ENTRY.ts, entryCode.ts); record the ids a real ctx drew, or give a real ctx a random recorded seed that its `random` uses · ctx, std-sessions · k.ctx std.sessions · S1 · 0092
- [x] 0214 red-team (rfc:0070): `*` in a redact path matches only digits, so no entry redacts a field under every `s.record` entry, and E2001 accepts the entry that does nothing; a key containing `.` cannot be addressed at all (repro: `redact(s.object({byId:s.record(s.object({card:s.string()}))}),{byId:{u1:{card:"4111"}}},{paths:["byId.*.card"],seed:1,salt:"store:pay"})` → card "4111"; `check` of `redact:['store:pay#byId.*.card']` over that store → no E2001); let `*` match a record key, or E2001 the path · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0092

## Red team 2026-11-09 (S1 batch 3)
- [x] 0232 red-team (rfc:0072), high: the largest-share rule makes the flood cheaper, not harder. A POST without a cookie mints a fresh caller (callerOf.ts), so 64 POSTs of about 300 bytes, each with a distinct `route.id`, fill 64 fingerprints from 64 callers. Past 64, `byShare` evicts the caller with the most bytes, which is the real session (it holds base state), and the flood keeps going until none is left: 20 KB of traffic, against about 5 MB before rfc:0072 (repro: ingest-caller.test.ts `boot`/`make`/`send`, one real POST with `state.blob` "s"×5000, then 64 cookieless `send(app, make("f"+i,"F",""))` with route `r${i}` → `listed` holds no real session). The conformance flood reuses one `flooder` cookie, so it never reaches the archive bounds, and the spec clause passes while the threat stays. Rank a share by sessions or fingerprints rather than bytes, or protect a caller's first session, and add a rotating-caller case · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0225 (2026-11-09: caller-based eviction removed, oldest `last` again)
- [x] 0233 red-team (rfc:0072), high: a known fingerprint's 5 kept sessions can still be pushed out. In `crowded`, every caller holding 1 session ties, and the oldest goes, so 5 cookieless POSTs of the same code, `data.at` and route replace all 5 real sessions (the repro gives `[[10,["spoof0".."spoof4"]]]`). An attacker needs only public facts: `keel.unexpected`, the element id from the DOM and the route id. THREAT-MODEL.md, ADR-0061 and RFC 0072 Known limits say "the kept sessions of other callers stay", which is false (repro: 5 × `send(make("real"+i,"keel.unexpected"))`, then 5 × cookieless `send(make("spoof"+i,"keel.unexpected"))` → `listed`). Keep the oldest sessions on a tie, or reserve slots for the earliest callers of a fingerprint · std-sessions, docs · std.sessions · S1 · 0226 (2026-11-09: as 0232; the fingerprint's 5 newest sessions remain replaceable by anyone posting matching values, THREAT-MODEL)
- [x] 0234 red-team (rfc:0072), high: `replayRun` makes a fresh `createCtx` with no std binding, so the replay recorder applies no `redact` entries. With an `error:keel.unexpected#data.at` entry, the live session records at `xxxxxxxxxxxxx` and the replay records `cart.checkout`. `reproduced` is then false for every honest session, so `keel session replay` gives E2004 and `minimize` and `to-test` stop. RFC 0072 says replay records "under the same std config" (repro, happy-dom: as `buggySession` with `bindStd(ctx,{"@keel/std-sessions":{cart:{redact:["error:keel.unexpected#data.at"]}}})` before `record`, then `replay(s,{routes:buggyRoutes()}).reproduced` → false). Bind the session's std config to the replay ctx, or carry the redaction entries in the session · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0226 (2026-11-09: `replay`/`minimize` take `redact`, `keel session` passes the serving modules' entries)
- [x] 0235 red-team (rfc:0072), medium: `redactionOf.error` runs `scrub` over every `data` string, and the fingerprint hashes the scrubbed `data.at`. One bug therefore gets a different fingerprint for each redacted value the user typed: with `el:cart.card#value`, typing "cart" gives `xxxx.checkout` (748e…) and "check" gives `cart.xxxxxout` (ffb0…), against 262c… otherwise. Each of these also fails replay. The masked `data.at`, set beside the public element id, also reveals the redacted value (repro: as 0234 with `redact:["el:cart.card#value"]` and card input "cart" then "") · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0234 (2026-11-09: `data.at` is never scrubbed; only an `error:` entry masks it)
- [x] 0236 red-team (rfc:0072), medium: `ingestSession` checks the 256 KiB limit on the posted session, then copies the first error event's error into `error`. A post with `error: null` and a 200 KB message in its error event is accepted, and the stored session is 400,342 bytes, so a stored session can be up to twice the "over 256 KiB 413" bound (repro: as 0232, `make` with message "m"×200_000 and posted `error: null` → status 200, `listed` session JSON 400 KB). Measure the recomputed session · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0225 (2026-11-09: the bound applies to the session as filed)
- [x] 0237 red-team (0227), medium: E2005 still misses `` import(`@keel/std-sessions/routes`) `` (a template literal), `import r = require('@keel/std-sessions/routes')`, `require(...)`, and a hop through a non-relative alias (`export * from '@app/ops'`). - [x] 0243 red-team (0095), medium: issue suppression. - [x] 0249 red-team (0095), low: volume and scheduling. There is no cap on issues created per run (up to 64 a night, accumulating across nights; GitHub's content-creation rate limit then fails the run with E1056). There is no `concurrency` group, so a `workflow_dispatch` during the 05:00 run lists the issues before either creates one, which gives duplicates. Scheduled workflows also stop silently after 60 days without repo activity (repro: `grep -n 'concurrency\|timeout' .github/workflows/sessions-nightly.yml` → none). ## Red team 2026-11-09 (E1303, sessions recording)
- [x] 0257 red-team (rfc:0073), high: the E1303 fix text leads straight to the leak. "keep the Secret in the payload; reveal it in a *.server.ts action, not in tx" moves `reveal` to the call site: a `*.server.ts` action doing `tx(setT, { t: reveal(p.token) })` puts the plaintext into the log, the state and `snapshot()`, which then reach `#k-state` (snapshotStores.ts) and storage (saveStores.ts). No rule fires: E1105 allows `reveal` in a server file, and E1303 (b) reads only transaction functions (repro: store/test style, `const copy = transaction(c, "copy", s.object({ t: s.string() }), (st, p) => ({ t: p.t }))`, `tx(login, { k: "PLAINTEXT" })`, then `tx(copy, { t: reveal(parsedLogPayload.k) })` → `c.snapshot()` holds `"PLAINTEXT"` in `state` and `log`; conformance `e1303([f("modules/app/c.server.ts", …that action…)])` → []). Reword the fix, and either flag a `reveal(...)` that flows into a `tx` payload or state the limit in k.store · check, store · k.store k.schema · S2 · 0239 (2026-11-09: fix text no longer suggests it; a revealed value copied into a plain string field is not detectable statically; design item)
- [x] 0258 red-team (rfc:0073), medium: E1303 (b) misses ordinary reducer shapes. Named reducers are not covered by the "helper" limit in the RFC: `function reduce(st, p) { return { t: reveal(p.k) } }` then `transaction(c, 'x', P, reduce)`; a reducer imported from `./red.ts`; `const r = reveal`; `const { reveal: r } = K` for `import * as K from '@keel/schema'`; `K['reveal'](…)`; `export { reveal as open } from '@keel/schema'` in a relative file; `St.transaction(...)` through `import * as St from '@keel/store'`. Each one gives 0 findings (repro: E1303 conformance helpers `e1303([f("modules/app/c.ts", S + R + ST + "function reduce(st, p) { return { t: reveal(p.k) } }\n…transaction(c, 'x', s.object({ k: s.secret(s.string()) }), reduce)\n")])` → []). Resolve an Identifier last argument to its local function declaration or const and its relative import, and track `reveal` aliases through const and destructuring · check · k.store · S2 · 0239 (2026-11-09: the runtime guard in `store()` is exact; the static gaps remain and are documented in the spec as partial) (commit 147f732b)
- [x] 0259 red-team (rfc:0073), medium: E1303 (a) resolution misses common schema shapes, so the rule acts as a static early warning, not a guard. Each of these gives 0 findings: spread shape `s.object({ ...base })`; computed key `{ ['t']: s.secret(…) }` (keyOf skips the value too); `s['secret'](…)`; `const { secret } = s`; `import * as K from '@keel/schema'` then `K.s.secret`; `export default s.object(…)` imported by default; `export { Inner as State }`; a barrel `export { State } from './State.ts'` or `export * from`; `import * as sch from './State.ts'` then `store(sch.State)`; `dev ? A : B`; `let State`; `mkStore(schema)` wrapping `store` (repro: as 0258, `f("modules/app/c.ts", S + ST + "const base = { t: s.secret(s.string()) }\nexport const c = store(s.object({ ...base }), {})\n")` → []). Make `store()` throw at construction when `hasSecret(schema.node)` (it is in @keel/schema internal). That closes every static miss at the first test run · check, store, schema · k.store · S2 · 0239 (2026-11-09: `store()` throws E1303 at construction on a state schema holding a secret; the static rule stays partial by design)
- [x] 0260 red-team (rfc:0073), medium: the runtime fallback is late and quiet. `snapshot()` throws only once a `Secret` value sits in state, so an optional or empty secret field passes every test, and the message "committed state or log no longer fits its schema" names neither the secret nor E1303. The throw then lands in places that hide it: the recorder's `here()` in `onEvent` throws before `append`, and `report` swallows it, so `fire` events drop out of the session silently and replay diverges. The dev inspector's `report()` (entryCode.ts) throws in `setInterval` every second and stops reporting every store, not only the one that failed. The server's snapshotStores and saveStores throw out of the render or action (repro: `store(s.object({ t: s.secret(s.string()).optional() }), {})` → `snapshot()` ok; after `tx(set, { t: "x" })` → throws the generic message). Covered by the construction-time throw proposed in 0259; until then, name `s.secret` in the message · store · k.store · S2 · 0259 (2026-11-09: construction guard makes the snapshot case unreachable; both messages name E1303 and the path)
- [ ] 0261 red-team (rfc:0073), low: whole-object copies get no static check and are coerced silently. `(st, p) => ({ ...st, ...p })` or `user: p.user`, where the payload object holds `s.secret` and the state object does not, compiles (a non-literal object has no excess-property check) and commits. The default open `s.object` keeps the `Secret` in live state under an undeclared key, and `snapshot()` drops it silently, so snapshot, restore and replay state differ from live state. Only a `closed: true` object rejects it (`unknown-key`), and `s.json()` rejects it (`type`). "State never holds a secret" (k.store) is false in memory (repro: store/test style, `store(s.object({ name: s.string() }), …)`, transaction payload `{ name, token: s.secret(s.string()) }` with `(st, p) => ({ ...st, ...p })` → committed value keys `["name","token"]`, `snapshot().state` `{ name: "a" }`) · store, schema · k.store k.schema · S2 · 0239
- [x] 0262 red-team (rfc:0073), low: E1303 (b) false positives with no fix and no scoped escape. A locally shadowed `const reveal = (x) => x` inside the reducer is reported, and so is `{ const store = (x) => x; store(s.object({ t: s.secret(…) })) }`. A compare-only reducer `(st, p) => ({ ...st, ok: hash(reveal(p.pin)) === st.pinHash })` stores no secret and is still reported (repro: as 0258, a reducer body `{ const reveal = (x) => x; return { t: reveal(p.k) } }` with payload `s.string()` → 1 finding). Resolve references by binding, not by name · check · k.store · S2 · 0239 (2026-11-09: shadowed locals skipped; compare-only reducers still fire, stated in the explain text)
- [x] 0263 red-team (sessions recording), low: the Home.ts comment says a test or replay "records nothing" because its origin is opaque. Under happy-dom `location.origin` resolves, so Home calls `record(ctx, { upload })` there. It is inert only because the to-test test and `replayRun` call `record` first, `record` returns the active recorder and drops the new `upload` without a word, and a sim ctx's `put` is a no-op (repro: in Home.ts, `signal<string>(path); if (upload !== null) endpoint.set(upload.href)` → `pnpm vitest run modules/ops/test/` in apps/sessions fails with E1201, so the branch ran). Checked and clean: the session carries no cookie (HttpOnly, never in the body), the href is rebuilt from declared params so no query token survives, the upload URL is origin plus path, and the emitted test holds only the fingerprint and seed and kills that mutation. 0251 holds as filed (recording.ts:136 `scope` is owned by the caller's render scope), but nothing reaches it in apps/sessions: the island is never disposed, since there is no client router. Fix the comment, and have `record` report options it ignored · apps, std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · 0251 (2026-11-09: comment corrected; apps/sessions/test/homeRecording.test.ts covers the live recorder and the upload URL)

## Red team 2026-11-09 (rfc:0074)
- [x] 0264 red-team (rfc:0074), high: a kept error skips value scrubbing. The recorder masks a matched `el:`/`tx:`/`route:` value in later error messages only once it has seen that value, and nothing reports `fire` while nothing observes, so an error kept before `record` reaches the session with the raw value in `message` and is uploaded (repro: the `pay.show` route of packages/std-sessions/test/redteam.test.ts, `bindStd` redact `el:pay.card#value`, `renderRoute`, `fire("pay.card", { type: "input", value: "4111222233334444" })`, click `pay.crash`, then `record(ctx).stop()` → error event message `card 4111222233334444 declined`; calling `record` before the input masks it). apps/sessions relies on exactly this path. Fix: errors delivered during `observe`'s replay keep `code` and `data.at` only (the recorder sets a flag around its `observe` call), or kept errors are not replayed to a recorder with redact entries · std-sessions, ctx · std.sessions k.ctx · S1 · 0256 (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: recorder learns current redacted values at start; a kept error whose field is gone keeps only code and data.at)
- [x] 0265 red-team (rfc:0074), medium: `detached` breaks two guarantees without `unsafe`. `uses` walks `parent`, so a detached scope inside a restricted component is unrestricted; and `registryOf` walks `parent`, so a detached scope under an isolated request scope registers stores in the module registry, where they outlive the request and are shared across requests (repro: store conformance `makeCart()`; `scope(() => { k.cart.uses([k.add]); tx(k.clear, {}) /* store.undeclared */; scope(() => tx(k.clear, {}).ok, { detached: true }) /* true */ })`; `const rq = scope(() => {}); isolate(rq); rq.run(() => scope(() => store(Cart, { items: [] }, { id: "rt.iso" }), { detached: true })); rq.dispose()` → `stores().has("rt.iso")` true). No check rule limits `detached`, and app code can use it to leak effects past route disposal. Fix: a check rule that refuses `detached` outside kernel and stdlib packages, or detached scopes that keep their creator's registry and `uses` declarations through a link other than `parent` · reactive, store, check · k.reactive k.store · S2 · none (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: E1207: detached scopes only in kernel and stdlib code)
- [x] 0266 red-team (rfc:0074), low: a detached scope's effect throw goes to whichever `set` or `tx` triggered the commit, past that caller's `onError`. The recorder's `effect(() => pull())` now has no handler, so a throw in `pull` would surface in the app's `tx` call (repro: `scope(() => { sig = signal(0) })`; `scope(() => effect(() => { if (sig.get() > 0) throw new Error("d") }), { detached: true })`; `scope(() => sig.set(1), { onError })` → `set` throws "d", `onError` is never called). Fix: the recorder passes an `onError` that records the throw in `errors()`, and k.reactive states where a detached effect's throw goes · reactive, std-sessions · k.reactive std.sessions · S2 · none (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: recorder scope has its own onError)
- [x] 0267 red-team (rfc:0074), low: the opaque-origin check reads `location.origin`, the URL's origin, not the document's (`self.origin`). In a `sandbox="allow-scripts"` frame `location.origin` is the server's, so `record` accepts the upload and the put gets 403 (`Origin: null`). On a Chrome `file:` page `location.origin` is `"file://"`, so Home's `URL.parse(path, location.origin)` gives `file:///sessions` (origin `"null"`), and `record` throws E2006 inside Home's render: the Boundary shows an error page instead of recording nothing. The `page === "null"` branch has no test, so removing it survives the suite (repro: `grep -rn 'opaque\|setURL' packages/std-sessions/{test,conformance}` → none; under happy-dom `happyDOM.setURL("file:///x")` gives `"null"`). Fix: compare with `self.origin`, test an opaque page, and keep a misconfigured upload from breaking a render · std-sessions, apps · std.sessions · S1 · none (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: self.origin, "null" opaque; Home degrades)
- [x] 0268 red-team (rfc:0074), low: E2006 accepts any URL with the page's origin, not only the ingest route: `new URL("blob:" + location.origin + "/0")`, a credentialed `https://u:p@host/sessions` (fetch refuses it with a TypeError, so status 0), and any same-origin path such as a redirecting `/logout?next=`. The real `put` uses fetch's default `redirect: "follow"`, so a 307 or 308 from that path re-posts the session body to another origin once that origin passes the preflight. `session(ctx).put` has no origin check at all (repro: happy-dom, `record(ctx, { upload: new URL("blob:" + location.origin + "/0") })` and the credentialed URL → no throw). Fix: `redirect: "error"` in the real `put`, and E2006 also refuses non-http(s) schemes and credentials · std-sessions · std.sessions · S1 · none (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: blob:, credentials refused; put checks origin, redirect error)
- [x] 0269 red-team (rfc:0074), low: kept errors are uploaded again by every recorder. A component that calls `record` on mount and `stop()` on dispose posts the same first-load error on every mount; each post raises the ingest `count`, and past 5 they replace that fingerprint's earlier sessions (repro: sim ctx, `nav`, one `error`, `for (i < 3) record(ctx, { upload: new URL("/sessions", location.origin) }).stop()` → `session(ctx).taken().length` 3). The RFC lists this as a known limit, but nothing bounds it. Fix: the ctx marks a kept error as uploaded, or the recorder remembers per ctx which kept errors it has put · std-sessions, ctx · std.sessions · S2 · none (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: taken errors remembered per ctx)
- [ ] 0270 red-team (rfc:0074), low: the merged "E2001-E2005 each fire on a fixture, not on its clean twin." clause is cited by five files, and the ledger (`buildLedger.ts`, `citesClause`) counts a clause covered when any one file cites it, so skipping or deleting `E2003-replay-input.test.ts` leaves the clause green and no gate notices that a rule lost its fixture (repro: rename that file to `.skip.ts` locally and rebuild the ledger: the clause stays covered). Fix: the ledger checks that each code a range clause names appears in a covering test's name, or the spec keeps one clause per code · cli, std-sessions · k.testing std.sessions · S2 · none
- [x] 0271 red-team (rfc:0074), low: copy and time. The std-sessions README says a recorder started "after the page's first navigation" gets the errors since it, but it gets those since the current `nav`, and any later `nav` clears them. Home.ts's doc comment has a line over 120 columns. A kept error is stamped when the recorder starts: one reported at 0 and recorded after `advance(60000)` has `at` 60000 and the session `at` 60000, and the base state is the post-error state, so `ingest --since` and the timeline move the error later than it happened (the fingerprint is unaffected). Fix: correct the README sentence, wrap the comment, and state in std.sessions that a replayed error's `at` is the recorder's start · std-sessions, apps · std.sessions · S2 · none (2026-11-09, rfc:0074: wording; kept errors stamped when reported)

## Red team 2026-11-10 (S2 Values)
- [x] 0319 router: high: the default-prefix elision is ambiguous when a prefixed route's second segment is a param, and E1509 allows that shape: beside `home` `/:locale` and `page` `/:locale/:slug` (`pathDefault` `en-US`), `routes.href('page', { locale: 'en-US', slug: 'de-DE' })` is `/de-DE`, which matches `home` with `de-DE`; `GET /en-US/de-DE` answers 308 to `/de-DE` (the German home), and `/en-US/en-US` 308s to `/en-US`, then to `/`. The page has no URL and the href/match identity fails for any slug, username or id equal to a locale value (repro: `compileRoutes` those two routes, `matchUrl(table, hrefIn(t, 'page', { locale: 'en-US', slug: 'de-DE' }, {}))` gives `home`). The property test only uses a static second segment (`/:p/items/:id`), so it cannot see it. Keep the default segment in `href` when the next written segment is a value of the prefix param, skip the step-2 308 in that case, and add a `/:p/:id` route to `prefix.property.test.ts` · router, server · k.prefix k.router · S2 · 0307
- [x] 0320 schema: medium: a format node with no in-memory implementation passes `check` on its base alone (`formats.get(node)?.test(x) === false` fails open): `s.object({ e: { ...Email, node: JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(Email)) } }).parse({ e: 'not an email' })` is `ok`. - [x] 0406 design context for the 0375 and 0385 RFCs (user discussion 2026-11-11; read from k.topology, k.server, k.runtime, nothing run): where a view renders is the deployable's adapter, not the route's code. (1) `node`: the server runs loaders and streams the same Keel components as HTML (not a template language); exists; `apps/sessions` is one node deployable with real pages. (2) resume, exists: the compiler marks only interactive subtrees as islands, the rest ships no JS; the page carries `#k-state` (store snapshots, island props, served route) and a script entry that restores stores and attaches handlers with no re-render and no loader re-run; every island resumes at load. Bundled only: `dist/<name>/` holds the client build and the server serves `/assets/*` itself (hashed files `immutable`); no option names a separate asset origin; a CDN in front as a cache is untested. Not checked: whether navigation after the first page stays in the browser. (3) `static`: pages prerendered at build; no params, no actions (E1553); a different thing from hosting a `client` build on a static host, and the 0375 RFC keeps the two names apart. (4) `client`: loaders, actions and views in the browser, built as an SPA, with `node` deployables answering JSON: the user's target shape ("koa API + angular on S3"); exists in part (golden-1 `web` + `api`), works on one origin only (`keel dev`, forwarding). Missing for (4): the static-host deploy path and CORS (0375), JSON endpoints without stub views (0385). Missing altogether: a node deployable serving a `client` deployable's SPA (`GET /` is 404), which 0375 adds as the provided default. User position: client-server separation is the main production shape; server-rendered pages stay allowed (not questioned as wrong); a page must never lose its `component` by accident. Out of scope unless the user asks: removing the `node` page path and resume · server, router, compiler, scaffold · k.topology, k.server, k.router, k.runtime · context for 0375, 0385

Found while verifying docs/THREAT-MODEL.md 2026-11-11; read from source, not reproduced. ## Red team 2026-11-16 (rfc:0103 std-credentials; findings and probes in tasks/0338-s8-reach/proposed/red-team-0103.md)
- [x] 0648 std-credentials (medium; a security choice for the user): an account whose stored hash is at a lower `ln` than the app (imported at 14, or not signed in since the app raised `ln`) answers a wrong password faster than an unknown account, which derives at the app cost: measured 20 ms against 170 ms at 14 under 17, and about 200 ms against 480 ms at 17 under 18. THREAT-MODEL states the window. A fix to weigh: after the stored derivation at `ln` s under an app at c, derive dummies at s, s+1, ..., c-1, so the work sums to 2^c; its cost is up to seven derivations and queue slots for one sign-in · std-credentials · spec · 2026-11-16: put to the user with the `ttl` and SHA-256 questions and answered "agree"; read as: the stated window stands, the padding is not built. Left open as a known limit the user may reopen (moot: password hashing is not in the framework)
- [x] 0649 std-credentials: E2201 misses `createRequire(import.meta.url)("@keel/std-credentials/server")`; E2202 misses a `*.server.ts` helper outside `modules/` (`lib/creds.server.ts`) that a module of a `client` or `static` deployable imports · std-credentials
- [x] 0650 check: a `topology.ts` whose argument is not a literal (`const t = { ... }; export default topology(t)`) gets no finding from `check`, and the rules that read the topology (E2202, the std-jobs rule) then report nothing · check · compiler
- [ ] 0651 std-db: in one root `pnpm test` (2026-11-16, load average 60) the `@keel/std-db` vitest worker died after its 38 tests passed: V8 `Fatal error ... Check failed: end > addr` in `WasmCodeAllocator::FreeCode` (Node 24.19, the PGlite WASM module); the next root run was green. If it returns: one fork for the PGlite files, or a newer Node · std-db · tooling · seen a second time on 2026-11-16 at 22:24 (a root `pnpm test` ended after 18 s with the same V8 `Fatal error`; the machine was quiet; the next run decides whether it repeats)
- [x] 0652 std-channels: `channelRoute` watches only the stores that are direct values of what its `stores` factory returns; a workflow's `stores(ctx)` holds its store under `.store`, so passing it whole watches nothing and says nothing (golden-1 missed `api.removal` saves that way, 0620). A finding, or a documented shape in keel-realtime · std-channels · skills · 2026-11-16: the shape is documented in the keel-realtime skill; no finding was added
- [x] 0653 check: a `topology.ts` in a subfolder that cannot be read statically is not a project root, so a workspace check folds that folder into the root project and reports no E1552 for it · check · 2026-11-16: the code matches the one written rule (a project root is a `topology.ts` that parses); counting an unreadable one changes `keel build` too (the folder leaves the root build), so it is the user's: tasks/0338-s8-reach/proposed/check-0653.md (a k.topology sentence, `projectDirs` in `@keel/compiler`); reproduced as an expected failure in `packages/check/test/topologyUnreadable.test.ts`

## RFC 0104 follow-ups (2026-11-16)
- [x] 0654 server: secrets made outside a request (startup config, a runner, a job) are in no log scrub set, and a runner's `failed` diag gets no scrub at all; RFC 0105 Questions 1 recommends entering the request scope around each runner `run` · server · spec · CLOSED 2026-11-17 (rfc:0107): each runner `run` enters its own scrub scope and its `failed` record is scrubbed; startup secrets are a stated limit (THREAT-MODEL)
- [ ] 0655 spec: k.server's `action` bullet lists the handler arguments as `{ params, url, caller, stores }` with no `bearer`, while k.api gives an API action one; k.server is at 1,494 of 1,500 tokens, so the words need room · spec
- [ ] 0656 spec: k.privacy says a `.pii` value of an action `output` is replaced by `[pii]` in a `diag` of a later throw in the same request; on an API route no loader runs after the action answers, so no conformance case reaches it. Reword it to what happens, or name the path that reaches it · spec · server
- [x] 0657 services: the client refuses a bearer by `/^[\w.~+/-]{1,4096}=*$/`, which allows more than 4,096 characters once `=` padding is counted; compare with the server's parse (k.api Bearer: a token68 of at most 4,096 characters) and make the two one rule · services · server · 2026-11-16: the reviewer compared them: both cap the token characters at 4,096 and leave trailing `=` uncounted, as k.api says; the only difference is that the server trims the header value. One rule
- [x] 0658 docs: `docs/VOICE.md` (48 commits), `docs/OUTPUT.md` (47) and `docs/A11Y-TARGET.md` (40) are close to the docs freshness limit of 50; re-verify each against the repo before it trips `pnpm check` · docs
- [x] 0659 spec: k.persist, k.prove, k.shell, std.db, std.workflows and std.shells are still `status: proposed`, and k.topology and k.hosting `implemented`, though each has conformance files that quote it and green suites; advance the ones whose suites cover every clause (`docs/freeze-review.md` lists them) · spec · 2026-11-16: audited clause by clause (tasks/0338-s8-reach/proposed/spec-status-0659.md): k.persist and k.prove moved to `conformance-covered`; k.topology and std.shells were moved and put back within the hour, since `pnpm ledger --diff` reported E1707 for both (0695); the rest is 0691 to 0694

## Red team 2026-11-16 (rfc:0105 and the rfc:0104 fixes; findings and probes in tasks/0338-s8-reach/proposed/red-team-0105.md)
- [x] 0666 server (high): a lone surrogate in a secret makes the scrub throw (`encodeURIComponent`, `URIError`), so a request with `{"k":"ab\ud800cd"}` in a secret field drops its `diag`, and on a live loader prints through `console.error` · server
- [x] 0667 server (medium): a `Secret` whose value is an object or an array marks none of the strings inside it · server · spec

THREAT-MODEL states it. Choose: E1111 covers `s.secret` in `params` and `search` as it covers `.pii`, or the `req` path is scrubbed, or both · check · server · spec · CLOSED 2026-11-17 (rfc:0107): E1111 covers `s.secret` in `params` and `search`, and the `req` path is scrubbed
- [x] 0669 server: overlapping marks leave part of a secret: the pii pass runs first, so a pii value inside a secret leaves the rest (`abcd-[pii]-9876`), and of two overlapping secrets the shorter can win (`[secret]-cccc`) · server
- [x] 0670 server: the scrub's regex is rebuilt for the message, each frame and the cause; a 660 KB request with 20,000 secret keys and 8 frames took 739 ms on the event loop; build it once per record or cap the set · server

- [x] 0672 server: a live stream's secret set grows without a cap while the stream is open (read from the code, not probed) · server · 2026-11-17 (day): probed (`test/liveStreamSecrets.test.ts`): the log already failed closed past the cap and the set grew by one value a tick. A request's set now stops growing once its forms are truly past the scrub's cap (`holdSecrets`): 858 values in the probe where it held 1,001; no spec change. A hard cap on the count of values is the other option, the user's: `tasks/0338-s8-reach/proposed/stream-secrets-0672.md`

## Red team 2026-11-16, third pass (the linear scan ab00816d; findings and probes in tasks/0338-s8-reach/proposed/red-team-0105c.md)
- [x] 0685 server (high): the scrub's cost is the number of forms times the text length: each long form gets its own pass over the whole text and a value has 3 to 6 forms, and the batched expressions for forms of 1,000 or fewer re-run wherever a batch matches. A 1,026,020-byte body (500 secret values of 1,009 characters and a 520,000-character field the thrown message holds) took 4,340 ms of synchronous work before the 500 came back; 256 values of 744 to 999 characters against a 400,000 run took 1,739 ms.
